(defvar byte-run--ssp-seen nil "Which conses/vectors/records have been processed in strip-symbol-positions?\nThe value is a hash table, the keys being the elements and the values being t.\n\nThe purpose of this is to detect circular structures.")
(defvar byte-run--ssp-seen nil "Which conses/vectors/records have been processed in strip-symbol-positions?\nThe value is a hash table, the keys being the elements and the values being t.\n\nThe purpose of this is to detect circular structures.")
nil
(defalias 'byte-run--strip-list #'(lambda (arg) "Strip the positions from symbols with position in the list ARG.\nThis is done by destructively modifying ARG.  Return ARG." (let ((a arg)) (while (and (not (gethash a byte-run--ssp-seen)) (progn (puthash a t byte-run--ssp-seen) (cond ((symbol-with-pos-p (car a)) (setcar a (bare-symbol (car a)))) ((consp (car a)) (byte-run--strip-list (car a))) ((or (vectorp (car a)) (recordp (car a))) (byte-run--strip-vector/record (car a)))) (consp (cdr a)))) (setq a (cdr a))) (cond ((symbol-with-pos-p (cdr a)) (setcdr a (bare-symbol (cdr a)))) ((or (vectorp (cdr a)) (recordp (cdr a))) (byte-run--strip-vector/record (cdr a)))) arg)))
(defalias (quote byte-run--strip-list) (function (lambda (arg) "Strip the positions from symbols with position in the list ARG.\nThis is done by destructively modifying ARG.  Return ARG." (let ((a arg)) (while (and (not (gethash a byte-run--ssp-seen)) (progn (puthash a t byte-run--ssp-seen) (cond ((symbol-with-pos-p (car a)) (setcar a (bare-symbol (car a)))) ((consp (car a)) (byte-run--strip-list (car a))) ((or (vectorp (car a)) (recordp (car a))) (byte-run--strip-vector/record (car a)))) (consp (cdr a)))) (setq a (cdr a))) (cond ((symbol-with-pos-p (cdr a)) (setcdr a (bare-symbol (cdr a)))) ((or (vectorp (cdr a)) (recordp (cdr a))) (byte-run--strip-vector/record (cdr a)))) arg))))
(quote byte-run--strip-list)

(let ((a arg)) (while (and (not (gethash a byte-run--ssp-seen)) (progn (puthash a t byte-run--ssp-seen) (cond ((symbol-with-pos-p (car a)) (setcar a (bare-symbol (car a)))) ((consp (car a)) (byte-run--strip-list (car a))) ((or (vectorp (car a)) (recordp (car a))) (byte-run--strip-vector/record (car a)))) (consp (cdr a)))) (setq a (cdr a))) (cond ((symbol-with-pos-p (cdr a)) (setcdr a (bare-symbol (cdr a)))) ((or (vectorp (cdr a)) (recordp (cdr a))) (byte-run--strip-vector/record (cdr a)))) arg)))
(defalias 'byte-run--strip-vector/record #'(lambda (arg) "Strip the positions from symbols with position in the vector/record ARG.\nThis is done by destructively modifying ARG.  Return ARG." (unless (gethash arg byte-run--ssp-seen) (let ((len (length arg)) (i 0) elt) (puthash arg t byte-run--ssp-seen) (while (< i len) (setq elt (aref arg i)) (cond ((symbol-with-pos-p elt) (aset arg i elt)) ((consp elt) (byte-run--strip-list elt)) ((or (vectorp elt) (recordp elt)) (byte-run--strip-vector/record elt))) (setq i (#{1+}# i))))) arg))
(defalias (quote byte-run--strip-vector/record) (function (lambda (arg) "Strip the positions from symbols with position in the vector/record ARG.\nThis is done by destructively modifying ARG.  Return ARG." (unless (gethash arg byte-run--ssp-seen) (let ((len (length arg)) (i 0) elt) (puthash arg t byte-run--ssp-seen) (while (< i len) (setq elt (aref arg i)) (cond ((symbol-with-pos-p elt) (aset arg i elt)) ((consp elt) (byte-run--strip-list elt)) ((or (vectorp elt) (recordp elt)) (byte-run--strip-vector/record elt))) (setq i (#{1+}# i))))) arg)))
(quote byte-run--strip-vector/record)
(function (lambda (arg) "Strip the positions from symbols with position in the vector/record ARG.\nThis is done by destructively modifying ARG.  Return ARG." (unless (gethash arg byte-run--ssp-seen) (let ((len (length arg)) (i 0) elt) (puthash arg t byte-run--ssp-seen) (while (< i len) (setq elt (aref arg i)) (cond ((symbol-with-pos-p elt) (aset arg i elt)) ((consp elt) (byte-run--strip-list elt)) ((or (vectorp elt) (recordp elt)) (byte-run--strip-vector/record elt))) (setq i (#{1+}# i))))) arg))
(defalias 'byte-run-strip-symbol-positions #'(lambda (arg) "Strip all positions from symbols in ARG.\nThis modifies destructively then returns ARG.\n\nARG is any Lisp object, but is usually a list or a vector or a\nrecord, containing symbols with position." (setq byte-run--ssp-seen (make-hash-table :test 'eq)) (cond ((symbol-with-pos-p arg) (bare-symbol arg)) ((consp arg) (byte-run--strip-list arg)) ((or (vectorp arg) (recordp arg)) (byte-run--strip-vector/record arg)) (t arg))))
(defalias (quote byte-run-strip-symbol-positions) (function (lambda (arg) "Strip all positions from symbols in ARG.\nThis modifies destructively then returns ARG.\n\nARG is any Lisp object, but is usually a list or a vector or a\nrecord, containing symbols with position." (setq byte-run--ssp-seen (make-hash-table :test (quote eq))) (cond ((symbol-with-pos-p arg) (bare-symbol arg)) ((consp arg) (byte-run--strip-list arg)) ((or (vectorp arg) (recordp arg)) (byte-run--strip-vector/record arg)) (t arg)))))
(quote byte-run-strip-symbol-positions)
(function (lambda (arg) "Strip all positions from symbols in ARG.\nThis modifies destructively then returns ARG.\n\nARG is any Lisp object, but is usually a list or a vector or a\nrecord, containing symbols with position." For each (PROP . VALUES) in a function's declaration,\nthe FUN corresponding to PROP is called with the function name,\nthe function's arglist, and the VALUES and should return the code to use\nto set this property.\n\nThis is used by `declare'.")

(defun make-obsolete (obsolete-name current-name when) "Make the byte-compiler warn that function OBSOLETE-NAME is obsolete.\nOBSOLETE-NAME should be a function name or macro name (a symbol).\n\nThe warning will say that CURRENT-NAME should be used instead.\nIf CURRENT-NAME is a string, that is the `use instead' message\n(it should end with a period, and not start with a capital).\nWHEN should be a string indicating when the function\nwas first made obsolete, for example a date or a release number." (put obsolete-name 'byte-obsolete-info (purecopy (list current-name nil when))) obsolete-name)

(put obsolete-name (quote byte-obsolete-info) (purecopy (list current-name nil when))) obsolete-name)

(defalias (quote make-obsolete) (function (lambda (obsolete-name current-name when) "Make the byte-compiler warn that function OBSOLETE-NAME is obsolete.\nOBSOLETE-NAME should be a function name or macro name (a symbol).\n\nThe warning will say that CURRENT-NAME should be used instead.\nIf CURRENT-NAME is a string, that is the `use instead' message\n(it should end with a period, and not start with a capital).\nWHEN should be a string indicating when the function\nwas first made obsolete, for example a date or a release number." (put obsolete-name (quote byte-obsolete-info) (purecopy (list current-name nil when))) obsolete-name)))

(defun make-obsolete-variable (obsolete-name current-name when &optional access-type) "Make the byte-compiler warn that OBSOLETE-NAME is obsolete.\nThe warning will say that CURRENT-NAME should be used instead.\nIf CURRENT-NAME is a string, that is the `use instead' message.\nWHEN should be a string indicating when the variable\nwas first made obsolete, for example a date or a release number.\nACCESS-TYPE if non-nil should specify the kind of access that will trigger\n  obsolescence warnings; it can be either `get' or `set'." (defmacro define-obsolete-variable-alias (obsolete-name current-name when &optional docstring) "Make OBSOLETE-NAME a variable alias for CURRENT-NAME and mark it obsolete.\n\nWHEN should be a string indicating when the variable was first\nmade obsolete, for example a date or a release number.\n\nThis macro evaluates all its parameters, and both OBSOLETE-NAME\nand CURRENT-NAME should be symbols, so a typical usage would look like:\n\n  (define-obsolete-variable-alias \\='foo-thing \\='bar-thing \"28.1\")\n\nThis macro uses `defvaralias' and `make-obsolete-variable' (which see).\nSee the Info node `(elisp)Variable Aliases' for more details.\n\nIf CURRENT-NAME is a defcustom or a defvar (more generally, any variable\nwhere OBSOLETE-NAME may be set, e.g. in an init file, before the\nalias is defined), then the define-obsolete-variable-alias\nstatement should be evaluated before the defcustom, if user\ncustomizations are to be respected.  The simplest way to achieve\nthis is to place the alias statement before the defcustom (this\nis not necessary for aliases that are autoloaded, or in files\ndumped with Emacs).  This is so that any user customizations are\napplied before the defcustom tries to initialize the\nvariable (this is due to the way `defvaralias' works).\n\nFor the benefit of Customize, if OBSOLETE-NAME has\nany of the following properties, they are copied to\nCURRENT-NAME, if it does not already have them:\n`saved-value', `saved-variable-comment'." (declare (doc-string 4) (indent defun)) `(progn (defvaralias ,obsolete-name ,current-name ,docstring) (dolist (prop '(saved-value saved-variable-comment)) (and (get ,obsolete-name prop) (null (get ,current-name prop)) (put ,current-name prop (get ,obsolete-name prop)))) (make-obsolete-variable ,obsolete-name ,current-name ,when)))
(defmacro define-obsolete-variable-alias (obsolete-name current-name when &optional docstring) "Make OBSOLETE-NAME a variable alias for CURRENT-NAME and mark it obsolete.\n\nWHEN should be a string indicating when the variable was first\nmade obsolete, for example a date or a release number.\n\nThis macro evaluates all its parameters, and both OBSOLETE-NAME\nand CURRENT-NAME should be symbols, so a typical usage would look like:\n\n  (define-obsolete-variable-alias \\='foo-thing \\='bar-thing \"28.1\")\n\nThis macro uses `defvaralias' and `make-obsolete-variable' (which see).\nSee the Info node `(elisp)Variable Aliases' for more details.\n\nIf CURRENT-NAME is a defcustom or a defvar (more generally, any variable\nwhere OBSOLETE-NAME may be set, e.g. in an init file, before the\nalias is defined), then the define-obsolete-variable-alias\nstatement should be evaluated before the defcustom, if user\ncustomizations are to be respected.  The simplest way to achieve\nthis is to place the alias statement before the defcustom (this\nis not necessary for aliases that are autoloaded, or in files\ndumped with Emacs).  This is so that any user customizations are\napplied before the defcustom tries to initialize the\nvariable (this is due to the way `defvaralias' works).\n\nFor the benefit of Customize, if OBSOLETE-NAME has\nany of the following properties, they are copied to\nCURRENT-NAME, if it does not already have them:\n`saved-value', `saved-variable-comment'." (defmacro eval-when-compile (&rest body) "Like `progn', but evaluates the body at compile time if you're compiling.\nThus, the result of the body appears to the compiler as a quoted\nconstant.  In interpreted code, this is entirely equivalent to\n`progn', except that the value of the expression may be (but is\nnot necessarily) computed at load time if eager macro expansion\nis enabled." (declare (debug (&rest def-form)) (indent 0)) (list 'quote (eval (cons 'progn body) lexical-binding)))

(defmacro with-suppressed-warnings (warnings &rest body) "Like `progn', but prevents compiler WARNINGS in BODY.\n\nWARNINGS is an association list where the first element of each\nitem is a warning type, and the rest of the elements in each item\nare symbols they apply to.  For instance, if you want to suppress\nbyte compilation warnings about the two obsolete functions `foo'\nand `bar', as well as the function `zot' being called with the\nwrong number of parameters, say\n\n(with-suppressed-warnings ((obsolete foo bar)\n                           (callargs zot))\n  (foo (bar))\n  (zot 1 2))\n\nThe warnings that can be suppressed are a subset of the warnings\nin `byte-compile-warning-types'; see the variable\n`byte-compile-warnings' for a fuller explanation of the warning\ntypes.  The types that can be suppressed with this macro are\n`free-vars', `callargs', `redefine', `obsolete',\n`interactive-only', `lexical', `mapcar', `constants' and\n`suspicious'.\n\nFor the `mapcar' case, only the `mapcar' function can be used in\nthe symbol list.  For `suspicious', only `set-buffer' and `lsh' can be used." (declare (debug (sexp body)) (indent 1)) (if (not (and (featurep 'macroexp) (boundp 'byte-compile--suppressed-warnings))) `(progn ,@body) (let ((byte-compile--suppressed-warnings (append warnings byte-compile--suppressed-warnings))) (macroexpand-all (macroexp-progn body) macroexpand-all-environment))))

(prog1 (defalias (quote with-suppressed-warnings) (cons (quote macro) (function (lambda (warnings &rest body) "Like `progn', but prevents compiler WARNINGS in BODY.\n\nWARNINGS is an association list where the first element of each\nitem is a warning type, and the rest of the elements in each item\nare symbols they apply to.  For instance, if you want to suppress\nbyte compilation warnings about the two obsolete functions `foo'\nand `bar', as well as the function `zot' being called with the\nwrong number of parameters, say\n\n(with-suppressed-warnings ((obsolete foo bar)\n                           (callargs zot))\n  (foo (bar))\n  (zot 1 2))\n\nThe warnings that can be suppressed are a subset of the warnings\nin `byte-compile-warning-types'; see the variable\n`byte-compile-warnings' for a fuller explanation of the warning\ntypes.  The types that can be suppressed with this macro are\n`free-vars', `callargs', `redefine', `obsolete',\n`interactive-only', `lexical', `mapcar', `constants' and\n`suspicious'.\n\nFor the `mapcar' case, only the `mapcar' function can be used in\nthe symbol list.  For `suspicious', only `set-buffer' and `lsh' can be used." (if (not (and (featurep (quote macroexp)) (boundp (quote byte-compile--suppressed-warnings)))) (backquote (progn #<<elisp-unquoted-form-type> form: body splice?: #t>)) (let ((byte-compile--suppressed-warnings (append warnings byte-compile--suppressed-warnings))) (macroexpand-all (macroexp-progn body) macroexpand-all-environment))))))) (progn :autoload-end (put (quote with-suppressed-warnings) (quote edebug-form-spec) (quote (sexp body)))) (function-put (quote with-suppressed-warnings) (quote lisp-indent-function) 1))

(defun byte-run--unescaped-character-literals-warning () "Return a warning about unescaped character literals.\nIf there were any unescaped character literals in the last form\nread, return an appropriate warning message as a string.\nOtherwise, return nil.  For internal use only." (if lread--unescaped-character-literals (let ((sorted (sort lread--unescaped-character-literals #'<))) (format-message "unescaped character literals %s detected, %s expected!" (mapconcat (lambda (char) (format "`?%c'" char)) sorted ", ") (mapconcat (lambda (char) (format "`?\\%c'" char)) sorted ", ")))))
